=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two strings s and pattern.
A string x is called almost equal to y if you can change at most one character in x to make it identical to y.
Return the smallest starting index of a substring in s that is almost equal to pattern. If no such index exists, return -1.
A substring is a contiguous non-empty sequence of characters within a string.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "abcdefg", pattern = "bcdffg"
Output: 1
Explanation:
The substring s[1..6] == "bcdefg" can be converted to "bcdffg" by changing s[4] to "f".

Example 2:

Input: s = "ababbababa", pattern = "bacaba"
Output: 4
Explanation:
The substring s[4..9] == "bababa" can be converted to "bacaba" by changing s[6] to "c".

Example 3:

Input: s = "abcd", pattern = "dba"
Output: -1

Example 4:

Input: s = "dde", pattern = "d"
Output: 0

 
Constraints:

1 <= pattern.length < s.length <= 10^5
s and pattern consist only of lowercase English letters.

 
Follow-up: Could you solve the problem if at most k consecutive characters can be changed?

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minStartingIndex(self, s: str, pattern: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Implementation:

def z_function(s):
    n=len(s)
    z=[0]*n
    l=r=0
    for i in range(1,n):
        if i<=r:
            z[i]=min(r-i+1, z[i-l])
        while i+z[i]<n and s[z[i]]==s[i+z[i]]:
            z[i]+=1
        if i+z[i]-1>r:
            l=i; r=i+z[i]-1
    return z

Then:

m=len(pattern); n=len(s)
combined = pattern + '#' + s
z = z_function(combined)
# L_i = z[m+1+i] for i in 0..n-m
# R_i via reversed:
rs = s[::-1]
rp = pattern[::-1]
rcombined = rp + '#' + rs
rz = z_function(rcombined)
# For start i, pos = n - i - m. R_i = rz[m+1+pos]? Wait combined reversed length m+1+n. Index in rz for rs position pos = m+1+pos. Yes.
for i in range(n-m+1):
    L = z[m+1+i]
    pos = n - i - m
    R = rz[m+1+pos]
    if L + R >= m - 1:
        return i
return -1

- If L_i >= m: exact.

Let's maybe write z_function with local variables for speed:
def z_function(t):
    n = len(t)
    z = [0] * n
    l = r = 0
    for i in range(1, n):
        if i <= r:
            zi = z[i-l]
            if zi > r - i + 1:
                zi = r - i + 1
        else:
            zi = 0
        while i + zi < n and t[zi] == t[i+zi]:
            zi += 1
        z[i] = zi
        if i + zi - 1 > r:
            l = i
            r = i + zi - 1
    return z
This is fine. Could use min.

Let's ensure code style:
class Solution:
    def minStartingIndex(self, s: str, pattern: str) -> int:
        def z_function(t: str):
            n = len(t)
            z = [0] * n
            l = r = 0
            for i in range(1, n):
                if i <= r:
                    z[i] = min(r - i + 1, z[i - l])
                while i + z[i] < n and t[z[i]] == t[i + z[i]]:
                    z[i] += 1
                if i + z[i] - 1 > r:
                    l = i
                    r = i + z[i] - 1
            return z

        m = len(pattern)
        n = len(s)

        z = z_function(pattern + '#' + s)
        rz = z_function(pattern[::-1] + '#' + s[::-1])

        for i in range(n - m + 1):
            left = z[m + 1 + i]
            pos = n - i - m
            right = rz[m + 1 + pos]
            if left + right >= m - 1:
                return i
        return -1

Let's maybe write a more optimized z_function:
def z_function(s):
    n = len(s)
    z = [0] * n
    left = right = 0
    for i in range(1, n):
        if i <= right:
            z[i] = min(right - i + 1, z[i - left])
        while i + z[i] < n and s[z[i]] == s[i + z[i]]:
            z[i] += 1
        if i + z[i] - 1 > right:
            left, right = i, i + z[i] - 1
    return z
This is standard. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is a hidden issue with "pattern.length < s.length" and if pattern
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def minStartingIndex(self, s: str, pattern: str) -> int:
        def z_function(t: str):
            n = len(t)
            z = [0] * n
            l = r = 0
            for i in range(1, n):
                if i <= r:
                    z[i] = min(r - i + 1, z[i - l])
                while i + z[i] < n and t[z[i]] == t[i + z[i]]:
                    z[i] += 1
                if i + z[i] - 1 > r:
                    l = i
                    r = i + z[i] - 1
            return z

        m = len(pattern)
        n = len(s)

        # z1[i] = LCP(pattern, s[i:])
        z1 = z_function(pattern + '#' + s)

        # z2[i] = LCP(reversed(pattern), reversed(s)[i:])
        z2 = z_function(pattern[::-1] + '#' + s[::-1])

        for i in range(n - m + 1):
            left = z1[m + 1 + i]
            pos = n - i - m
            right = z2[m + 1 + pos]

            if left + right >= m - 1:
                return i

        return -1
```
